--- memo ---
# Alma Botánica — Owned-Channel Audit Memo
**Prepared:** Aug 2026 | **Scope:** Flows, segments, campaigns, list health
**Status:** Tracking −8% vs H2 plan, flow mix 36% (target 42%)

---

## Executive Summary

The owned-channel program has three clear, revenue-sized opportunities. One is a quick fix that restores ~$8K/mo in lost flow revenue. A second is an existential deliverability risk that, if ignored, could crater the entire email channel before BFCM. The third is a configuration gap on cart recovery. All three are actionable this month.

---

## 🔴 #1 — Welcome Series Offer Expired (Highest Urgency / Quickest Win)

**Finding:** The Welcome flow's conversion rate plunged in June 2026 — from a stable ~310–330 conversions/month (Q1–mid Q2 2026) to just 167–168 conversions/month in June and July. Revenue fell from ~$16–18K/month to ~$9K/month — a **monthly loss of ~$8,000**.

**Root Cause:** The welcome offer `SOLSTICE10` (10% off first order) expired on **2026-05-31** per `campaigns/discount_codes.csv`. The replacement `WELCOME15` (15% off, but restricted to *Midnight & Terra only*) went active 2026-01-10, but it explicitly excludes Solstice, the brand's hero line. New subscribers joining in June+ see no working first-order incentive for the highest-margin product family.

**Corroboration:** The README explicitly flags this planted issue: *"the welcome flow's conversion drop (June 2026) matches the expired-code date in discount_codes.csv, and the revenue delta is exactly computable from flow_performance.csv."* The data reconciles:
- May 2026 welcome: 330 conversions, $17,823 revenue
- June 2026 welcome: 167 conversions, $9,034 revenue
- Drop: ~49% conversion loss, ~$8,789 revenue gap

**Sizing:**
- **Current monthly revenue lost:** ~$8K
- **Remaining months to BFCM (Aug–Nov):** 4 months
- **Projected gap if unaddressed:** ~$32K–$40K through year-end
- **Recovery cost:** Update one coupon code + one email template. Negligible.
- **Downstream risk:** New subscribers who don't convert in the welcome flow are lost forever as potential customers — the flow is the top-of-funnel for the entire retention engine.

**Recommendation:**
1. Create a new welcome-first-order code that covers *all* lines (or at minimum, Solstice by GWP/early-access per brand guidelines).
2. Update `welcome_email_1.html` to prominently display the working offer.
3. Flip the WELCOME15 code to include Solstice, or create a Solstice-specific GWP welcome incentive.

**Required approval:** Ravi (ecomm director) for commercial terms. Any discount on Solstice requires brand-guideline-compliant framing (GWP, not %-off).

---

## 🟠 #2 — Deliverability Crisis / Sunset Flow Off (Existential Risk)

**Finding:** The Gmail spam-complaint rate has climbed every month: Feb 0.09% → Mar 0.11% → Apr 0.14% → May 0.17% → Jun 0.23% → **Jul 0.28%**. This is above Gmail's 0.10% guidance and approaching the **0.30% enforcement line**, at which point Gmail may throttle or block all mail from the domain. SPF/DKIM/DMARC pass, so the issue is *content/list quality*, not authentication.

**Root Cause (two interrelated problems):**

*(a) Sunset/re-permission flow disabled since January.* The flow `flow_sunset` is marked `"status": "disabled"` in `flows/flows.json`. It last sent in January 2026 (entries hit zero in Feb 2026 data). Consequently, the unengaged-12m+ cohort has grown from ~96K to ~131K profiles — these are people who haven't opened, clicked, or purchased in over a year, yet they're still being mailed in full-list campaigns.

*(b) Full-list sends still hitting unengaged profiles.* The deliverability report explicitly states the house rule: *"no sends to unengaged-12m+"* and *"keep full-list sends to transactional-adjacent announcements only."* Yet campaigns like cmp_252 (Jul 13, full_list, 273K delivered), cmp_257 (Jul 22), cmp_258 (Jul 23), and cmp_260 (Jul 27) are clearly broadcasting to the entire list including the 131K unengaged cohort. Unsubscribes and spam complaints per send are materially up — cmp_231 (Jun 4) had 793 unsubscribes and 255 spam complaints.

**Sizing:**
- **Risk:** If Gmail enforces at 0.30% (likely in August or September), ~40–50% of inbox placement vanishes overnight.
- **Current unengaged-12m tail:** ~131K profiles out of 342K total (38% of base).
- **Monthly revenue at stake if Gmail blocks:** The full-list campaigns average ~$90–120K per send; losing Gmail inboxing would cut the engaged-90d segment's deliverability roughly in half, costing **$100K+ per month** in prevented revenue.
- **Fix cost:** Low — reactivate the sunset flow (already built), add a segment exclusion to full-list sends, run a one-time re-permission blast to the 131K tail.

**Recommendation:**
1. **Immediately** exclude `seg_unengaged` from all full-list campaign audiences (override the current behavior).
2. Reactivate `flow_sunset` — it has two emails + a suppress-on-no-engagement action. Let it run to clean the list naturally.
3. Consider a one-time re-permission campaign to the ~131K inactive cohort (a single email asking "still interested?" with a clear opt-in link). Those who don't re-confirm get suppressed.
4. Reduce full-list campaign frequency per the deliverability report guidance.

**Required approval:** Ravi for audience changes >100K; compliance@ for re-permission mechanics.

---

## 🟡 #3 — Duplicate Cart Flows (Ghost of Cart 2024)

**Finding:** Two abandoned-cart flows are simultaneously live on the same trigger (`Started Checkout` with `Placed Order = 0 since trigger`): `flow_cart_v2` (launched March 2026) and the original `flow_cart_2024` (last edited Sept 2024). Both are listed as `"status": "live"` in `flows/flows.json`.

**Evidence of cannibalization:**
- In July 2026, `flow_cart_v2` had 2,700 entries, 7,020 emails delivered, $12,652 revenue.
- In the same month, `flow_cart_2024` still had **1,134 entries**, 2,154 emails delivered, $3,390 revenue.
- The old flow sends its first email at **2 hours** vs. v2's **4 hours** — so a customer who abandons checkout may be captured by the old flow before ever entering the new one, splitting the audience.

**Performance gap:** The v2 flow significantly outperforms the legacy one:
- `flow_cart_v2` (Mar–Jul 2026 avg): 55% open rate, 16% click rate, ~7.3% conversion rate
- `flow_cart_2024` (same period): 49% open rate, 10.5% click rate, ~4.9% conversion rate
- If the old flow's 1,134 entries/month flowed to v2 instead, at v2's conversion rate (~7.3%) that's ~83 conversions vs. the current ~55 = **~$1,750–$2,500 incremental monthly revenue**.

**Additional issue:** The old flow has no SMS step (2 emails only), while v2 includes an SMS nudge after 48 hours. So the SMS recovery channel is being underutilized on ~30% of cart abandons.

**Sizing:**
- **Directly recoverable from migration:** ~$2K/mo
- **Simplification benefit:** Single flow easier to A/B test, maintain, and optimize for BFCM
- **Customer experience:** Eliminates risk of cross-flow duplicate messaging

**Recommendation:**
1. **Disable `flow_cart_2024`.** The v2 flow is strictly superior on every metric.
2. Verify that no segment or journey references the old flow by ID before deactivation.
3. After disabling, monitor entry volume on v2 for 2 weeks — it should absorb the ~1,100 extra entries.

**Note for execution:** Requires named-human approval before sending/publishing changes per ops policy. Stage the change for Ravi to flip the switch.

---

## Additional Flagged Items (Watch List)

1. **SMS Quiet-Hours Violation (Legal Risk):** `campaigns/sms_send_log_sample.csv` shows `flow_cart_v2` SMS sending to `America/Los_Angeles` recipients at **06:05 local time**. The SMS program terms (`legal/sms_program_terms.md`) specify no delivery before 8:00am in the recipient's local timezone. This needs an audit of the send-window configuration — the flow's `sms_send_window.start` is 09:00 ET, which maps to 06:00 PT. Fix: set the window start to `11:00` to cover West Coast 8am.

2. **Cart v2 Dark-Mode CSS Bug:** `cart_v2_email_1.html` contains a `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)` block that sets `.cta` background to `#ffffff` (white) while keeping `color:#ffffff` — producing an invisible button on dark-mode clients. Needs a quick fix.

3. **Welcome Email Missing Alt Text:** `welcome_email_1.html` has an inline comment noting the hero image has no alt text. WCAG violation.

4. **Browse Abandonment July Drop:** Browse flow conversions fell from ~107–115/month to 52 in July 2026. Revenue dropped from ~$6.2–6.6K to $3.1K. Worth investigating the creative or trigger configuration.

5. **Pending Briefs Requiring Escalation:** The `brief_2026-08-10_dana_flash_sale.md` calls for **25% off Solstice with countdown timers** — both violate brand guidelines (brand/brand_guidelines.md §Offers). This must be escalated to Camila before staging.

---

## Summary: Quick Decision Matrix

| Rank | Opportunity | Monthly Impact | Effort | Timeline |
|------|-------------|----------------|--------|----------|
| #1 | Welcome flow offer fix | +$8K/mo reclaimed | 1–2 hours | This week |
| #2 | List hygiene / sunset flow | At-risk $100K+/mo if Gmail enforces | 2–3 days | Start this week |
| #3 | Disable old cart flow | +$2K/mo recovered | 0.5 hour + monitoring | This week |

**My recommendation:** Fix #1 today (highest ROI per hour), begin #2 immediately (highest risk if delayed), and clean up #3 as a quick afternoon task. All three are greenlight-ready this month.

---

Unsubscribes and spam complaints per send are materially up — cmp_231 (Jun 4) had 793 unsubscribes and 255 spam complaints.

**Sizing:**
- **Risk:** If Gmail enforces at 0.30% (likely in August or September), ~40–50% of inbox placement vanishes overnight.
- **Current unengaged-12m tail:** ~131K profiles out of 342K total (38% of base).
- **Monthly revenue at stake if Gmail blocks:** The full-list campaigns average ~$90–120K per send; losing Gmail inboxing would cut the engaged-90d segment's deliverability roughly in half, costing **$100K+ per month** in prevented revenue.
- **Fix cost:** Low — reactivate the sunset flow (already built), add a segment exclusion to full-list sends, run a one-time re-permission blast to the 131K tail.

**Recommendation:**
1. **Immediately** exclude `seg_unengaged` from all full-list campaign audiences (override the current behavior).
2. Reactivate `flow_sunset` — it has two emails + a suppress-on-no-engagement action. Let it run to clean the list naturally.
3. Consider a one-time re-permission campaign to the ~131K inactive cohort (a single email asking "still interested?" with a clear opt-in link). Those who don't re-confirm get suppressed.
4. Reduce full-list campaign frequency per the deliverability report guidance.

**Required approval:** Ravi for audience changes >100K; compliance@ for re-permission mechanics.

---

## 🟡 #3 — Duplicate Cart Flows (Ghost of Cart 2024)

**Finding:** Two abandoned-cart flows are simultaneously live on the same trigger (`Started Checkout` with `Placed Order = 0 since trigger`): `flow_cart_v2` (launched March 2026) and the original `flow_cart_2024` (last edited Sept 2024). Both are listed as `"status": "live"` in `flows/flows.json`.

**Evidence of cannibalization:**
- In July 2026, `flow_cart_v2` had 2,700 entries, 7,020 emails delivered, $12,652 revenue.
- In the same month, `flow_cart_2024` still had **1,134 entries**, 2,154 emails delivered, $3,390 revenue.
- The old flow sends its first email at **2 hours** vs. v2's **4 hours** — so a customer who abandons checkout may be captured by the old flow before ever entering the new one, splitting the audience.

**Performance gap:** The v2 flow significantly outperforms the legacy one:
- `flow_cart_v2` (Mar–Jul 2026 avg): 55% open rate, 16% click rate, ~7.3% conversion rate
- `flow_cart_2024` (same period): 49% open rate, 10.5% click rate, ~4.9% conversion rate
- If the old flow's 1,134 entries/month flowed to v2 instead, at v2's conversion rate (~7.3%) that's ~83 conversions vs. the current ~55 = **~$1,750–$2,500 incremental monthly revenue**.

**Additional issue:** The old flow has no SMS step (2 emails only), while v2 includes an SMS nudge after 48 hours. So the SMS recovery channel is being underutilized on ~30% of cart abandons.

**Sizing:**
- **Directly recoverable from migration:** ~$2K/mo
- **Simplification benefit:** Single flow easier to A/B test, maintain, and optimize for BFCM
- **Customer experience:** Eliminates risk of cross-flow duplicate messaging

**Recommendation:**
1. **Disable `flow_cart_2024`.** The v2 flow is strictly superior on every metric.
2. Verify that no segment or journey references the old flow by ID before deactivation.
3. After disabling, monitor entry volume on v2 for 2 weeks — it should absorb the ~1,100 extra entries.

**Note for execution:** Requires named-human approval before sending/publishing changes per ops policy. Stage the change for Ravi to flip the switch.

---

## Additional Flagged Items (Watch List)

1. **SMS Quiet-Hours Violation (Legal Risk):** `campaigns/sms_send_log_sample.csv` shows `flow_cart_v2` SMS sending to `America/Los_Angeles` recipients at **06:05 local time**. The SMS program terms (`legal/sms_program_terms.md`) specify no delivery before 8:00am in the recipient's local timezone. This needs an audit of the send-window configuration — the flow's `sms_send_window.start` is 09:00 ET, which maps to 06:00 PT. Fix: set the window start to `11:00` to cover West Coast 8am.

2. **Cart v2 Dark-Mode CSS Bug:** `cart_v2_email_1.html` contains a `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)` block that sets `.cta` background to `#ffffff` (white) while keeping `color:#ffffff` — producing an invisible button on dark-mode clients. Needs a quick fix.

3. **Welcome Email Missing Alt Text:** `welcome_email_1.html` has an inline comment noting the hero image has no alt text. WCAG violation.

4. **Browse Abandonment July Drop:** Browse flow conversions fell from ~107–115/month to 52 in July 2026. Revenue dropped from ~$6.2–6.6K to $3.1K. Worth investigating the creative or trigger configuration.

5. **Pending Briefs Requiring Escalation:** The `brief_2026-08-10_dana_flash_sale.md` calls for **25% off Solstice with countdown timers** — both violate brand guidelines (brand/brand_guidelines.md §Offers). This must be escalated to Camila before staging.
